\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.20757pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.20757pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.8375pt\kern-3.04367pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.3125pt\kern-2.49922pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}
HA
Let

\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$},\ \scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{y}}}{}$}\,\}})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\,\}})

and consider the communicating system

S=(\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}})_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}\in\{\,\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}},{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}\,\}}\quad\text{whose synchronous semantics is}\quad{\llbracket S\rrbracket}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\,\}})

It is easy to check that
S
is lock-free.
However,
S
is not harmonic, since

\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\,\cat\,\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{y}}}{}$}\in S(\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}})
and there is no

w\in{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
such that

w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}=\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{y}}}{}$}
.

HA
\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.20757pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.20757pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.8375pt\kern-3.04367pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.3125pt\kern-2.49922pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}
LF
Let us consider the
system
S=(\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}})_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}\in\{\,\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}},{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\,\}}

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\,\}})

\displaystyle\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\,\}})

\displaystyle\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$},\ \scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\,\}})

It is easy to check that

{\llbracket S\rrbracket}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$},\ \scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\,\}})

and that
S
is harmonic.
However,
S
is not lock-free.
In fact, by taking
w=\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\in{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
, we
have that
w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}
is not maximal in
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}
, since

\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}=w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}
.
However, there is no word
w^{\prime}
such that

w\cat w^{\prime}\in{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
and

w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}\neq\varepsilon
.

SF
\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.20757pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.20757pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.8375pt\kern-3.04367pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.3125pt\kern-2.49922pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}
LF
Immediate, since
otherwise, by

\mathsf{LF}\implies\mathsf{DF}
proved above, we would get

\mathsf{SF}
to imply
\mathsf{DF}
, which we showed not to hold.

Let us consider

S=(\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}})_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}\in\{\,\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}},{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\,\}}
where

\displaystyle\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,(\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$})^{n}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat(\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$})^{\omega}\;\big|\;n\in\mathbb{N}\,\}\cup\{\,(\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$})^{\omega}\,\}})

\displaystyle\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,(\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$})^{\omega}\,\}})

\displaystyle\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}

It is easy to check that

It is not difficult also to check that
S
is lock-free.
However, it is not starvation-free.
In fact, by taking the non maximal word
w=\varepsilon
, we
have that for the infinite word

w^{\prime}=(\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$})^{\omega}
and for the participant

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}

w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}}
is non maximal and

ww^{\prime}\in{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
.
However
w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}}=\varepsilon
.

\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.20757pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.20757pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.8375pt\kern-3.04367pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.3125pt\kern-2.49922pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}

Let

S=(\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}})_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}\in\{\,\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}},{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}},\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\,\}}\quad\text{whose synchronous semantics is}\quad{\llbracket S\rrbracket}=\{\,\varepsilon,\ \scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$},\ \scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\,\}

However,
S
is not deadlock-free, since for the maximal and
finite word
\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\in S
we have that

w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}}
(
=\varepsilon
) is not maximal in

\mathbb{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}_{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}
.

DF
\wedge
SF
\implies
SLF
In order to show SLF, let

w\in{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
be finite and let
w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}}}
be
non maximal in
S(\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}})
for
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{P}}
.
Besides, let
w^{\prime}
be such that
ww^{\prime}
is maximal in

{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
.
We have to show that
w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}}}\neq\varepsilon
.
We distinguish two cases:

w^{\prime}
is finite:

Since
S
is DF we can infer that

w^{\prime}\neq\varepsilon
and
w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}}}\neq\varepsilon
.

w^{\prime}
is infinite:

We get

w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}}}\neq\varepsilon
immediately by SF.

SLF
\implies
DF
\wedge
SF
To show DF by contradiction, let us
assume to have
w\in{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
finite and maximal and such
that
w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}}}
is non maximal in
S(\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}})
for

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{P}}
.
By SLF we have that
w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}}}\neq\varepsilon
for each

w^{\prime}
such that
ww^{\prime}
is maximal in
{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
.
Since we assumed
w
to be maximal, the only
possible
w^{\prime}
is
\varepsilon
, contradicting
w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}}}\neq\varepsilon
.
To show SF by contradiction, let us assume to have

w\in{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
finite and such that
w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}}}

is non maximal in
S(\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}})
for
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{P}}
.
Besides, let us assume that there exists an infinite
w^{\prime}
such
that
ww^{\prime}\in{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
and

w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}}}=\varepsilon
.
Since
w^{\prime}
is infinite,
ww^{\prime}
is trivially maximal
in
{\llbracket S\rrbracket}
and we get a contradiction with SLF.
∎

5.
Communication Properties by Construction

Harmonicity (cf.
Section

4
) is the only property
guaranteed on any system obtained via projection.
This is a simple consequence of
Section

3
.

Corollary
.

If
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
is a g-language then

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
is harmonic.

Proof.

By
Section

3
,

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}\subseteq{\llbracket\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}\rrbracket}
. Now, by monotonicity of
projection, we get

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}\subseteq{\llbracket\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}\rrbracket}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
, that is
harmonicity of
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
.
∎

To ensure the other properties on a system
{\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}}
we need
to require some conditions on the g-language
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
.
Basically, we will strengthen CUI which is too weak.
For instance,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{cui}}({\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}})
does imply neither deadlock-freedom nor
lock-freedom for
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
.

Example

(CUI
\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\displaystyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 3.75pt\kern-5.27776pt$\textstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 2.625pt\kern-4.45831pt$\scriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{\kern 1.875pt\kern-3.95834pt$\scriptscriptstyle\not$\hss}{\implies}}}
DF, LF)
.

It is easy to check that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{cui}}({\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}})
holds for the g-language

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,w,w^{\prime}\,\}})
where

w=\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{l}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\qquad\text{and}\qquad w^{\prime}=\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{r}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}

Informally,
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
is CUI because
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}
can ascertain
which of its last actions to execute from the first input.
So,
Sections

3
and
3
ensure that

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}={\llbracket\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}\rrbracket}
.
However,
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
is not deadlock-free.
In particular,
w\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}={\llbracket\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}\rrbracket}
is a
deadlock since it is a finite maximal word whose projection on
{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}
,
namely
w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}=\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}
, is not maximal in

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}
because

w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}=\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}
.

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
is non lock-free either by

Section

4
.

\diamond

In many models (cf.
[
HLV+16
]
) in order to ensure, besides
other properties, also the correctness of
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
, a
condition called
well-branchedness
is required.
We identify a notion weaker than well-branchedness, which by analogy
we dub
branch-awareness
(BA for short).

Definition

(Branch-awareness)
.

A participant
{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}

distinguishes
two g-words

w_{1},w_{2}\in\Sigma_{\text{int}}^{\infty}
if

{w_{1}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}\neq{w_{2}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}\quad\text{ and }\quad{w_{1}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}\not\prec{w_{2}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}\quad\text{ and }\quad{w_{2}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}\not\prec{w_{1}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}.

A g-language
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
on
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{P}}
is
branch-aware
if each

{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{P}}
distinguishes all maximal words in

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
whose projections on
{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}
differ.

Example
.

The language
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{pref}}({\{\,w,w^{\prime}\,\}})
with

w=\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{l}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}
and

w^{\prime}=\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{r}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}
from
Section

5
is not branch-aware, since

w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}=\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}
and

w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}=\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{?}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{${\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}{\,}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}{!}}{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}{}$}
, hence

w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}\neq w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}
but

w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}\prec w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}}}
.

\diamond

Condition
{w_{1}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}}}}\neq{w_{2}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}}}}

in
Section

5
is not strictly needed to define BA, but it makes the
notion of ‘distinguishes’ more intuitive.
Equivalently, as shown in
Section

5
below, a participant

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}
distinguishes two branches if, after a common prefix,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}
is
actively involved in both branches, performing different interactions.

Proposition
.

Participant
{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}
distinguishes two g-words

w_{1},w_{2}\in\Sigma_{\text{int}}^{\infty}
iff there are

w^{\prime}_{1}\cat\alpha_{1}\preceq w_{1}
and

w^{\prime}_{2}\cat\alpha_{2}\preceq w_{2}
such that

{w^{\prime}_{1}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}}}}={w^{\prime}_{2}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}}}}
and

{\alpha_{1}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}}}}\neq{\alpha_{2}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}}}}
.

Proof.

Trivial.
∎

The notions of well-branchedness in the
literature
[
HLV+16
]
additionally impose that

{\alpha_{1}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}
and
{\alpha_{2}}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}
in the above
proposition are input actions, but for a (unique) participant (
a.k.a.
the
selector
) which is required to have different outputs.
In our case the notion of selector corresponds to the “first”
participant that distinguishes two words.
Also in our case a selector must be involved in each branch but, due
to the perfect symmetry of input and output actions in synchronous
communications, its involvement can happen through input or output
actions.
This is illustrated by the following example.

Example

(Selector and input actions)
.

Consider the words

w=\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\qquad\text{and}\qquad w^{\prime}=\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}$}

The participant
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}
immediately distinguishes
w
and

w^{\prime}
via its first actions, that is the input from
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}
and the output to
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}
.
Hence,
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}
is the selector of the branch made of
w
and

w^{\prime}
.
Notice that
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}
and
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}
also distinguish these two words,
however this happens “later”.
Finally, observe that the same would hold if we replace

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}

with

\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{C}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{B}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{m}}}

in
w^{\prime}
.

\diamond

In our theory, BA is not needed for correctness, but it is
nevertheless useful to prove the communication properties presented in

Section

4
.

Theorem

(Consequences of BA)
.

Let
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
be a branch-aware and CUI sc-language. Then

{\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}}
satisfies all the properties in

Sections

4
,
4
,
4
,
4
and
4
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
be a branch-aware sc-language such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{cui}}({\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}})
holds.
We prove the properties separately.

Harmonicity
Immediate by
Section

5
.

Lock-freedom
By contradiction, let us assume

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
not to be lock-free.
By
Section

4
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{cui}}({\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}})
, it follows that there exist a
participant
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{P}}
and a finite g-word
w\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}

such that

•

w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}
is not maximal in
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}
;

•

for all
w^{\prime}
if
w\cat w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
then

w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}=\varepsilon
.

By
Section

5
,
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
is harmonic. Hence, by the
above and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{cui}}({\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}})
, there exists
w^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
such that

•

w^{\prime\prime}\neq w
;

•

w^{\prime\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}=w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}
;

•

there is
\hat{w}
such that

w^{\prime\prime}\cat\hat{w}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
and

\hat{w}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}\neq\varepsilon
.

This means that, by taking a maximal extension of
w
and a
maximal extension of
w^{\prime\prime}\hat{w}
, we would get two non
branch-aware words in
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
, contradicting our hypothesis of

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
being branch-aware.

Deadlock-freedom
Immediate by

Section

4
.

Starvation-freedom
By contradiction, let us assume

\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
not to be starvation-free.
By
Section

4
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{cui}}({\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}})
, it follows that there exist a
participant
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{P}}
and a finite g-word
w\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}

such that

•

w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}
is not maximal in
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}
;

•

there is an infinite word
w^{\prime}
such that

w\cat w^{\prime}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
and

w^{\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}=\varepsilon
.

Now, by harmonicity of
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{}}}}
(
Section

5
), non
maximality of
w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}
and by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.5}\textsf{cui}}({\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}})
, it follows that
there exist
w^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
and a finite word
\hat{w}

such that

•

w^{\prime\prime}\hat{w}\in\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
;

•

w^{\prime\prime}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}=w{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}
;

•

\hat{w}{\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}}}\neq\varepsilon
.

The above means that by taking
ww^{\prime}
and any maximal
extension of
w^{\prime\prime}\hat{w}
we would get two maximal words
in
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
which
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{A}}
cannot distinguish, so contradicting our
hypothesis of
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
being branch-aware.

Strong lock-freedom
Immediate since

\mathsf{SLF}=\mathsf{SF}\wedge\mathsf{DF}
.
∎

Example

(Task dispatching and branch-awareness)
.

In order to show that the g-language
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
in

Section

3
is branch-aware, we first notice that each
maximal word in
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
ends with the interactions

\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{D}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{s}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{H}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{s}}}$}
. If
\mathcal{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0}L}
were not
branch-aware, there should be two maximal words

and

w^{\prime}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{D}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{s}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{H}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{s}}}$}
and a

such that

(w\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{D}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{s}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{H}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{s}}}$}){\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}\prec(w^{\prime}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{D}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{s}}}$}\cat\scalebox{.8}{$\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{S}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\xrightarrow{\scriptstyle{}}}\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{H}}\!{\color[rgb]{0,0.6,0}\colon}\!\!{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{s}}}$}){\downarrow}_{{\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{X}}}}
. This is impossible, since
w
and

w^{\prime}
are both generated by the non terminal symbol
L^{\prime}
and
hence cannot contain the message
\mathsf{\color[rgb]{0.72,0,0}{s}}
.

\diamond
